Let me first establish my blind marks. My blind readings give FINDINGS, QUOTE, OPEN, SPLIT for each case. I need to derive BLIND MARK from those.

The mark rules:
1. CASE DISPUTED: situation itself decides a point against the fixed verdict (before any theory).
2. DISAGREE: on at least one point, the theory's verdict reaches a different finding from the fixed verdict.
3. SPLIT: on at least one point, the theory's sentences support two readings that reach different findings, and the text leaves the choice open.
4. SILENT: on at least one point, the theory leaves the finding open (takes deciding matter as input unstated, or sentences stop short).
5. AGREE: on every point, the theory's verdict reaches the same finding.

Note the order: CASE DISPUTED first, then DISAGREE, then SPLIT, then SILENT, then AGREE. So if a case has both a SPLIT and a SILENT on different points, the mark is SPLIT (it comes first in the order). Wait, "the first rule that fits" - so the order is CASE DISPUTED > DISAGREE > SPLIT > SILENT > AGREE.

"## Scope, and a question that can be wrong

A contract is a declared subset of the physically admitted changes, and a stated scope is what makes it one.
